- BUG/ASK (owner 2026-11-01): "How are patron palettes being picked? It doesn't feel random. I got Sal colored Pete like five times last round." Finding: the picks are uniform over each venue's (look, palette) list (mirror.c sal_combo, build/looks_pick.h), but the random byte comes from an 8-bit xorshift (main.c next_rand) seeded once at power-on (0xA7, main_cold.c) and never mixed with timing: same sequence every power-on, only 255 states, some strides reach fewer pairs. -> FIXED + merged 11-01 (title.c rng_mix at the start key, mirror.c spawn pick XOR DIV; test ROMs keep fixed seeds via ROM byte 0x3FFD; tests/test_rng.py). Measured: fixed sequence per power-on, each pair followed by ~5 of 50, chi-sq 290 -> 51-74. Owner's exact Pete streak not reproduced (cause or chance).

Answered: SML2-style means DMG map art from a fixed tile set that fits in VRAM, so scrolling writes only the map (1 byte per cell) and never streams tile pixels (16 bytes each). Q88 widened to a) leave, b) trim 10 zones, c) a fixed-set DMG redraw (about 682 tiles down to about 240). Also to measure: whether the map can give the BG more VRAM slots than 176 (few sprites on the map).

SML2 world: 64x64 cells, 240 distinct tiles, 142 used once, one 384-tile set loaded once, 8800 addressing. Ours (DMG preview): 3072 cells, 658 distinct, 499 used once. The difference is that our buildings are each one big unique picture.

- 11-06 reference survey (a read-only agent): the best DMG ideas are for map streaming.
  1. A pop-based drain in vq_exec: about 25% more bytes per VBlank, small job.
  2. An HBlank bulk copy (libbet popslide, a reuse repo under zlib, so it needs attribution): 8 B per line, so 40 lines gives about 20 more tiles a frame; costs CPU against the map guard; check music ticks.
  3. Fill-run packets.
  The rest is already done (spread BG refresh, cold code out of ROM0) or of little use. Plan: try 1, then 2, on the map speed branch before any Q88 art work, and measure. The owner's Q87 and Q88 are still open.

Would it be better to simplify the center city so that it uses less unique tiles?"
  - Numbers (DMG, read-only count): 658 unique map tiles. Land (ground, decor, road) is only 84 of them; venues 587; town 99 (93 only there, 72 used once). Only 1 land pair is equal under a recolour on DMG (the DMG has one BG palette, so a palette swap cannot share a DMG tile; snowy and green pines are already different pixels). 27 land pairs differ by 6 px or less. Most crowded 21x19 view: 175 of 176 slots (saloon 53, space 60, zoo 35, town 26, land 20); worst without the town: 157.
  - Plan: a gb-artist proposal (no import): (1) consolidate land tiles: one shared set of shapes per texture, recoloured per biome on Color, merged near-duplicates on DMG; (2) the town square redrawn as a simpler map piece of its own (no story-art tiles, no pour-scene glass), keeping its character (memory map-buildings-unique-character). Q90 asked: spread the venues apart (recommended: wait and measure first).
- Owner 11-06: "with Sal's walking animation on the map I would like it to match the walking animation in game. Right now it's very jittery up and down very fast. In the game, he doesn't move up and down with every single frame. There's a delay, so he bobs more slowly." Cause: the map pose changes every 4 px moved (smap.c:322 and 351), so on Color it flips about every 2 frames; levels step every WALK_STEP_FRAMES (10). Plan: a gb-worker paces the map walk poses and bob by time, 10 frames a step, DMG and Color.
- Owner 11-06: "I know that there's a greater number of tiles, and that consolidating them doesn't fix the overwhelming number of tiles. But it strikes me that it would reduce the number of tiles that has to be swapped in during the scroll. if the Tiles from one venue are freed up before another venue comes into view. Then, based on the direction that the player is walking, you can begin loading in the tiles from the next venue before you get there. That should give you some frames to load in tiles before you actually need them. Since you know which venue is going to come into view whenever the player presses a directional button, shouldn't we be able to predict which tiles we're going to need to load? Since the map is not free roam, can't we predict what we're going to need to unload and load?"
  - Now: the engine loads only 1 line (8 px) ahead plus the next stretch near a corner (smap_priv.h WALK_AHEAD 24, LEAD 1), and picks slots at run time (the free-slot search). Deeper look-ahead needs spare slots, which the crowded views lack (175 of 176), so it pairs with the tile reduction.
  - Plan (owner's direction): a build-time ROUTE PLAN per stretch and direction (tiles to drop, tiles to load, their slots, loaded early in frames with spare time); it replaces the run-time slot search. - Owner 11-06 (Q95, Q96, map cohesion), verbatim: "Venues:igloo and milk bar both option 1. but give me a couple variations of option one for the milk bar. For the zoo, I want to use option one, but I would like for you to integrate a couple of the accessory ideas from option two. For the mountain, option two is closer, although it should be on the other side and wider than it currently is. It seems like you're trying to restrict it to fit in the space that's currently there, but the mountain is much more important than the military base as far as the design goes. So you could make the military base tiny and have the mountain dominate the scene. We don't necessarily need the road that's in front of it, the airstrip that's in front of it. You could make a miniature airstrip somewhere in the art for it if you wanted to, but I don't even know that we absolutely need it. And the whole mountain itself could be wider than the space you give it now. I don't think you necessarily have to limit it to the size that it currently is. It also might be good if there are some accessory mountain tiles that we could put next to those mountains so that it looked like it was in a mountain range. Those mountain tiles might also be helpful up around the igloo to imply that it's maybe snowy up there because it's in the mountains. Finally, I think we can reverse the position of some of the other items so that things are more logical on the map. So, for example, we could put the igloo up in the right top corner instead of having the lodge there. So the igloo could come as like the character travels up into the mountains past the bistro, and then as they come down out of the mountain area, they're at the lodge. And then if you went down a little bit farther, you could start running into some of the other things. The transition from the lodge into the factory needs to have some logic to it, though. It's not often that you see industrial factories next to lodges. And so I don't know if there's maybe a river that goes between the two, and the logs float down and are used in the factory, or what we do logically, but there needs to be some kind of sense in the map as to why there would be a factory randomly there. We need to think about that in location flow from venue to venue. What's the logic of this flow so that as they transition from one to another, it's not just some kind of jarring transition. I don't know that we need a large desert space up above the saloon. The saloon could be on the edge of the desert, so kind of where the outskirts of the city meets the desert from below. The mountain could be large, kind of in the middle. There could be kind of a lake tile with maybe the path going over a bridge over the lake so that we end up having the lake at the tea house like we'd had in previous versions. That sort of thing. That's the kind of cohesiveness that I'm looking for in the map. One other thing with the stadium, at some point along the way, we had a venue design that actually had the baseball field. Right now, the stadium location doesn't really read as a stadium, and the scoreboard at the top of it reads more like a sign than a scoreboard. I was never actually thrilled about the scoreboard in the venue stage. You know, I only know it's a scoreboard because you told me it was a scoreboard. And so I definitely don't want it being like a sign over the top of the venue."
  - Plan: (1) gb-artist art round: milk bar = option 1 plus 2-3 variations; zoo = option 1 with a couple of option 2's accessories (giraffe, bamboo, fence); mountain = option 2's twin-peak style, on the other side of the base, much wider and dominant, the base tiny, no airstrip road (a tiny airstrip optional), plus mountain-range accessory tiles (also snowy ones for the igloo's mountains); stadium redesigned as a baseball park with a field (earlier round 2/3 mockups had one: c1d9c374, 29234190), no sign-like scoreboard. Igloo option 1 is picked. (2) layout round A4 for flow logic: igloo top right in the mountains, past the bistro; down out of the mountains to the lodge; a river from the lodge carrying logs to the factory (or another reason the factory is there); saloon on the desert edge where the town's outskirts meet the desert, below the town, no big desert above it; the mountain large in the middle; a lake by the tea house with the path on a bridge; every venue-to-venue transition has a reason. The owner's flow puts the igloo before the lodge (bistro, igloo, lodge, factory): asked Q97 to confirm the level order. The venue-stage scoreboard: owner was never thrilled with it (noted, no task yet).

- 2026-11-07 Color tile cap investigation (agent a1db0bc, 7aacb109, docs/dev/notes/color_tile_cap_2026-11-07.md): bit 15 of the Color cell word is unused; the Color engine stores slot+variant nibbles, not ids, so an 11-bit Color id costs 0 B WRAM (my earlier "22 B" was wrong: that table is DMG-only). Second ceiling: one ROM bank holds ~1018 Color tiles (16 KB), so 1023 would fail at link. Route A (recommended): flip-merge mirrored Color tiles via CGB attribute flip bits: ~11% fewer ids on main's set, 0 WRAM, 0 ROM0, ~8 cycles per new cell, Color only behind is_cgb. Route B: 11-bit id + second tile bank. Build: WRAM_LO 3936/4096, WRAM_HI_0 3635/4096, ROM0 477 B free (linker map). Measuring Route A on the real import map next.

Tile identity is the index pattern only, so one tile CAN be shown in several palettes, but in practice only 50 of 934 Color ids are (mostly plain ground; id 0 in 8 palettes). Art is drawn per biome, so shared shapes (pines, peaks, rocks, bushes) are mostly separate tiles: the big untapped saving.
